.nobold{font-weight:normal}</style><span class="nobold">(<a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">PPP</a>)</span></th><td class="infobox-data">2019<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>&#160;estimate</td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-pol-div-fake-li">•&#160;Total</div></th><td class="infobox-data">$4.78 billion</td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-pol-div-fake-li">•&#160;Per capita</div></th><td class="infobox-data">$73,600</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a>&#160;<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r886047488"/><span class="nobold">(nominal)</span></th><td class="infobox-data">2020&#160;estimate</td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-pol-div-fake-li">•&#160;Total</div></th><td class="infobox-data">$5.61 billion<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>&#32;(<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal)">160th</a>)</td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-pol-div-fake-li">•&#160;Per capita</div></th><td class="infobox-data">$85,347&#32;(<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">7th</a>)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">HDI</a>&#160;<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r886047488"/><span class="nobold">(2013)</span></th><td class="infobox-data">0.888<br /><span class="nowrap"><span style="color:darkgreen">very high</span></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Currency</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_dollar" title="Cayman Islands dollar">Cayman Islands dollar</a> (<a href="/wiki/ISO_4217" title="ISO 4217">KYD</a>)</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Time_zone" title="Time zone">Time zone</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/UTC-5:00" class="mw-redirect" title="UTC-5:00">UTC-5:00</a> (<a href="/wiki/Eastern_Time_Zone" title="Eastern Time Zone">EST</a>)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Date format</th><td class="infobox-data">dd/mm/yyyy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic" title="Left- and right-hand traffic">Driving side</a></th><td class="infobox-data">left</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_Cayman_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Telephone numbers in Cayman Islands">Calling code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/North_American_Numbering_Plan" title="North American Numbering Plan">+1</a>-<a href="/wiki/Area_code_345" title="Area code 345">345</a></td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Postal codes in the Pitcairn Islands">UK postcode</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><div class="postal-code">KYx-xxxx</div></td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:KY" title="ISO 3166-2:KY">KY</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.ky" title=".ky">.ky</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Website</th><td class="infobox-data"><a rel="nofollow" class="external free" href="https://www.gov.ky/">https://www.gov.ky/</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <b>Cayman Islands</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/eɪ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;face&#39;">eɪ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ən/: &#39;on&#39; in &#39;button&#39;">ən</span></span>/</a></span></span>) is a self-governing <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">British Overseas Territory</a>, and the largest by population. The 264-square-kilometre (102-square-mile) territory comprises the three islands of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman" title="Grand Cayman">Grand Cayman</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Brac" title="Cayman Brac">Cayman Brac</a> and <a href="/wiki/Little_Cayman" title="Little Cayman">Little Cayman</a>, which are located to the south of Cuba and northeast of <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>, between <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a> and Mexico's <a href="/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n_Peninsula" title="Yucatán Peninsula">Yucatán Peninsula</a>. The capital city is <a href="/wiki/George_Town,_Cayman_Islands" title="George Town, Cayman Islands">George Town</a> on Grand Cayman, which is the most populous of the three islands.
</p><p>The Cayman Islands is considered to be part of the geographic <a href="/wiki/Western_Caribbean_Zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Western Caribbean Zone">Western Caribbean Zone</a> as well as the <a href="/wiki/Greater_Antilles" title="Greater Antilles">Greater Antilles</a>. The territory is a major world <a href="/wiki/Offshore_financial_centre" title="Offshore financial centre">offshore financial centre</a> for international businesses and wealthy individuals, largely as a result of the state not charging taxes on any income earned or stored.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With a GDP per capita of $91,392, the Cayman Islands has the highest standard of living in the Caribbean.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Immigrants from over 130 countries and territories reside in the Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="History of the Cayman Islands">History of the Cayman Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Jamaica" title="Colony of Jamaica">Colony of Jamaica</a>, and <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_Federation" title="West Indies Federation">West Indies Federation</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png/220px-2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="111" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png/330px-2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png/440px-2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png 2x" data-file-width="2660" data-file-height="1346" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2_dollar_Cayman_1975.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>2 dollar Cayman 1975</div></div></div>
<p>No evidence of an indigenous people has been found on the Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> It is believed that they were discovered by <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> on 10 May 1503 during his final voyage to the Americas.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Cayman_Islands_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Cayman_Islands-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> He named them 'Las Tortugas' after the large number of turtles found there (which were soon hunted to near-extinction);<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Cayman_Islands_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Cayman_Islands-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> however, in the succeeding decades the islands began to be referred to as Caimanas or Caymanes, after the <a href="/wiki/Caiman" title="Caiman">caimans</a> present there.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Cayman_Islands_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Cayman_Islands-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> No immediate colonisation followed Columbus's discovery, but a variety of settlers from various backgrounds eventually came, including <a href="/wiki/Pirate" class="mw-redirect" title="Pirate">pirates</a>, shipwrecked sailors, and deserters from <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Cromwell" title="Oliver Cromwell">Oliver Cromwell</a>'s army in <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Sir <a href="/wiki/Francis_Drake" title="Francis Drake">Francis Drake</a> briefly visited the islands in 1586.<sup id="cite_ref-keytocayman.com_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-keytocayman.com-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town,_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/220px-Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/330px-Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/440px-Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2728" data-file-height="1926" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_National_Museum_-_George_Town,_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cayman Islands National Museum, George Town, Grand Cayman</div></div></div>
<p>The first recorded permanent inhabitant, Isaac Bodden, was born on <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman" title="Grand Cayman">Grand Cayman</a> around 1661. He was the grandson of an original settler named Bodden, probably one of Oliver Cromwell's soldiers at the taking of Jamaica in 1655.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">England</a> took <a href="/wiki/English_overseas_possessions" title="English overseas possessions">formal control</a> of the Cayman Islands, along with Jamaica, as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Madrid_(1670)" title="Treaty of Madrid (1670)">Treaty of Madrid of 1670</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> That same year saw an attack on a turtle fishing settlement on Little Cayman by the Spanish under <a href="/wiki/Manuel_Ribeiro_Pardal" title="Manuel Ribeiro Pardal">Manuel Ribeiro Pardal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-keytocayman.com_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-keytocayman.com-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Following several unsuccessful attempts at settlement in what had by now become a haven for pirates,<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> a permanent English-speaking population in the islands dates from the 1730s.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> With settlement, after the first royal land grant by the <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Jamaica" class="mw-redirect" title="Governor of Jamaica">Governor of Jamaica</a> in 1734, came the perceived need for slaves.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Many were brought to the islands from Africa; this is evident today with the majority of native Caymanians being of African and/or English descent.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The results of the first census taken in the islands in 1802 showed the population on Grand Cayman to be 933, with 545 of those inhabitants being enslaved.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Slavery was abolished in the Cayman Islands in 1833. At the time of abolition, there were over 950 people of African ancestry enslaved by 116 families.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Cayman_Islands_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Cayman_Islands-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 22 June 1863, the Cayman Islands became officially declared and administered as a dependency of the Crown Colony of Jamaica.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The islands continued to be governed as part of the <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Jamaica_(1655%E2%80%931962)" class="mw-redirect" title="Colony of Jamaica (1655–1962)">Colony of Jamaica</a> until 1962 when they became a separate <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">Crown colony</a> while Jamaica became an independent <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_realm" title="Commonwealth realm">Commonwealth realm</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg/220px-Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg/330px-Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg/440px-Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1150" data-file-height="738" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Heroes_Square_of_George_Town.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Heroes Square in the centre of George Town, which commemorates the Cayman Islands' war dead. The Legislative Assembly building is at the left.</div></div></div>
<p>On 8 February 1794, the Caymanians rescued the crews of a group of ten <a href="/wiki/Merchant_ship" title="Merchant ship">merchant ships</a>, including HMS <i>Convert</i>, an incident that has since become known as the <a href="/wiki/Wreck_of_the_Ten_Sail" title="Wreck of the Ten Sail">Wreck of the Ten Sail</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Cayman_Islands_12-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Cayman_Islands-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The ships had struck a reef and run aground during rough seas.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Legend has it that <a href="/wiki/George_III_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="George III of Great Britain and Ireland">King George III</a> rewarded the island with a promise never to introduce taxes as compensation for their generosity, as one of the ships carried a member of the King's own family. While this remains a popular legend, the story is not true.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 1950s, tourism began to take off with the opening of Owen Roberts International Airport (ORIA) in 1952,<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> a bank and several hotels, plus a number of scheduled flights and cruise stop-overs.<sup id="cite_ref-keytocayman.com_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-keytocayman.com-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Politically the Cayman Islands were an internally self-governing territory of Jamaica from 1958 to 1962; however, they reverted to direct British rule following the <a href="/wiki/Independence_of_Jamaica" title="Independence of Jamaica">independence of Jamaica</a> in 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> In 1972, a large degree of internal autonomy was granted by a new constitution, with further revisions being made in 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The Cayman Islands government focused on boosting the territory's economy via tourism and off-shore finance, both of which mushroomed from the 1970s onwards.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands_14-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_the_Cayman_Islands-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The Cayman Islands have historically been a tax-exempt destination, and the government has always relied on indirect and not direct taxes. The territory has never levied <a href="/wiki/Income_tax" title="Income tax">income tax</a>, <a href="/wiki/Capital_gains_tax" title="Capital gains tax">capital gains tax</a>, or any <a href="/wiki/Wealth_tax" title="Wealth tax">wealth tax</a>, making them a popular <a href="/wiki/Tax_haven" title="Tax haven">tax haven</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-biswas_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-biswas-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitution was further modified in 2001 and 2009, codifying various aspects of human rights legislation.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 11 September 2004 the island of Grand Cayman, which lies largely unprotected at sea level, was hit by <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Ivan" title="Hurricane Ivan">Hurricane Ivan</a>, creating an 8-foot (2.4&#160;m) <a href="/wiki/Storm_surge" title="Storm surge">storm surge</a> which flooded many areas of Grand Cayman.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_13-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 83% of the dwellings on the island were damaged including 4% requiring complete reconstruction. A reported 70% of all dwellings suffered severe damage from flooding or wind. Another 26% sustained minor damage from partial roof removal, low levels of flooding, or impact with floating or wind-driven hurricane debris.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Power, water and communications were disrupted for months in some areas as Ivan was the worst hurricane to hit the islands in 86 years.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Grand Cayman began a major rebuilding process and within two years its infrastructure was nearly returned to pre-hurricane status. Due to the tropical location of the islands, more hurricanes or tropical systems have affected the Cayman Islands than any other region in the Atlantic basin; it has been brushed or directly hit, on average, every 2.23 years.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_Map.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Cayman_Islands_Map.svg/330px-Cayman_Islands_Map.svg.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="164" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Cayman_Islands_Map.svg/495px-Cayman_Islands_Map.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Cayman_Islands_Map.svg/660px-Cayman_Islands_Map.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="502" data-file-height="249" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_Map.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of the Cayman Islands, showing the three main islands about 120 kilometres (75 miles) apart</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Geography of the Cayman Islands">Geography of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The islands are in the western <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Sea" title="Caribbean Sea">Caribbean Sea</a> and are the peaks of an <a href="/wiki/Undersea_mountain_range" title="Undersea mountain range">undersea mountain range</a> called the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Ridge" title="Cayman Ridge">Cayman Ridge</a> (or Cayman Rise). This ridge flanks the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Trough" title="Cayman Trough">Cayman Trough</a>, 6,000&#160;m (20,000&#160;ft) deep<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> which lies 6&#160;km (3.7&#160;mi) to the south.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The islands lie in the northwest of the Caribbean Sea, east of <a href="/wiki/Quintana_Roo" title="Quintana Roo">Quintana Roo</a>, Mexico and <a href="/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n_(state)" class="mw-redirect" title="Yucatán (state)">Yucatán State</a>, Mexico, northeast of Costa Rica, north of Panama, south of Cuba and west of Jamaica. They are situated about 700&#160;km (430&#160;mi) south of <a href="/wiki/Miami,_Florida" class="mw-redirect" title="Miami, Florida">Miami</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> 750&#160;km (470&#160;mi) east of Mexico,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> 366&#160;km (227&#160;mi) south of Cuba,<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> and about 500&#160;km (310&#160;mi) northwest of <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman" title="Grand Cayman">Grand Cayman</a> is by far the largest, with an area of 197&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (76&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Grand Cayman's two "sister islands", <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Brac" title="Cayman Brac">Cayman Brac</a> and <a href="/wiki/Little_Cayman" title="Little Cayman">Little Cayman</a>, are about 120&#160;km (75&#160;mi) east north-east of Grand Cayman and have areas of 38 and 28.5&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (14.7 and 11.0&#160;sq&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> respectively. The nearest land mass from Grand Cayman is the <a href="/wiki/Canarreos_Archipelago" title="Canarreos Archipelago">Canarreos Archipelago</a> (about 240&#160;km or 150 miles away), whereas the nearest from the easternmost island Cayman Brac is the <a href="/wiki/Jardines_de_la_Reina" title="Jardines de la Reina">Jardines de la Reina</a> archipelago (about 160&#160;km or 100 miles away)&#160;– both of which are part of Cuba.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Waterfront,_George_Town,_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/220px-Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/330px-Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg/440px-Waterfront%2C_George_Town%2C_Grand_Cayman.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="533" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Waterfront,_George_Town,_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>George Town waterfront.</div></div></div>
<p>All three islands were formed by large <a href="/wiki/Coral_heads" class="mw-redirect" title="Coral heads">coral heads</a> covering submerged ice-age peaks of western extensions of the Cuban <a href="/wiki/Sierra_Maestra" title="Sierra Maestra">Sierra Maestra</a> range and are mostly flat. One notable exception to this is <a href="/wiki/The_Bluff_(Cayman_Islands)" title="The Bluff (Cayman Islands)">The Bluff</a> on Cayman Brac's eastern part, which rises to 43&#160;m (141&#160;ft) above sea level, the highest point on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The terrain is mostly a low-lying <a href="/wiki/Limestone" title="Limestone">limestone</a> base surrounded by <a href="/wiki/Coral_reef" title="Coral reef">coral reefs</a>. The portions of <a href="/wiki/Prehistoric" class="mw-redirect" title="Prehistoric">prehistoric</a> <a href="/wiki/Coral_reef" title="Coral reef">coral reef</a> that line the coastline and protrude from the water are referred to as <i>ironshore</i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Blue_iguana_(Cyclura_lewisi)_male.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG/220px-Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="97" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG/330px-Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG/440px-Blue_iguana_%28Cyclura_lewisi%29_male.JPG 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="2412" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Blue_iguana_(Cyclura_lewisi)_male.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Blue iguana</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="List of mammals of the Cayman Islands">List of mammals of the Cayman Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="List of birds of the Cayman Islands">List of birds of the Cayman Islands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_butterflies_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="List of butterflies of the Cayman Islands">List of butterflies of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Mammal" title="Mammal">mammalian</a> species in the Cayman Islands include the introduced <a href="/wiki/Dasyprocta_punctata" class="mw-redirect" title="Dasyprocta punctata">Central American agouti</a><sup id="cite_ref-MSW3_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MSW3-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Bats_of_the_Caribbean#Grand_Cayman" class="mw-redirect" title="Bats of the Caribbean">eight species of bats</a>. At least three now extinct <a href="/wiki/List_of_rodents_of_the_Caribbean#Cayman_Islands" title="List of rodents of the Caribbean">native rodent species</a> were present until the discovery of the islands by Europeans. Marine life around the island of the Grand Cayman includes <a href="/wiki/Tarpon" title="Tarpon">tarpon</a>, silversides (<i><a href="/wiki/Atheriniformes" title="Atheriniformes">Atheriniformes</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/French_angelfish" title="French angelfish">French angelfish</a> (<i>Pomacanthus paru</i>), and <a href="/wiki/Giant_barrel_sponge" title="Giant barrel sponge">giant barrel sponges</a>. A number of <a href="/wiki/List_of_cetacean_species" class="mw-redirect" title="List of cetacean species">cetaceans</a> are found in offshore waters. These species include the <a href="/wiki/Ziphius_cavirostris" class="mw-redirect" title="Ziphius cavirostris">goose-beaked whale</a> (<i>Ziphius cavirostris</i>), <a href="/wiki/Blainville%27s_beaked_whale" title="Blainville&#39;s beaked whale">Blainville's beaked whale</a> (<i>Mesoplodon densirostris</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Sperm_whale" title="Sperm whale">sperm whale</a> (<i>Physeter macrocephalus</i>).
</p><p>Cayman <a href="/wiki/Birds" class="mw-redirect" title="Birds">avian fauna</a> includes two <a href="/wiki/Endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Endemic">endemic</a> subspecies of <i><a href="/wiki/Amazona" class="mw-redirect" title="Amazona">Amazona</a></i> parrots: <i>Amazona leucocephala hesterna</i> or <a href="/wiki/Cuban_amazon" title="Cuban amazon">Cuban amazon</a>, presently restricted to the island of <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Brac" title="Cayman Brac">Cayman Brac</a>, but formerly also on <a href="/wiki/Little_Cayman" title="Little Cayman">Little Cayman</a>, and <i>Amazona leucocephala caymanensis</i> or <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman_parrot" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Cayman parrot">Grand Cayman parrot</a>, which is native to the Cayman Islands, forested areas of Cuba, and the <a href="/wiki/Isla_de_la_Juventud" title="Isla de la Juventud">Isla de la Juventud</a>. Little Cayman and Cayman Brac are also home to <a href="/wiki/Red-footed_booby" title="Red-footed booby">red-footed</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown</a> boobies.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Although the barn owl (<i><a href="/wiki/Tyto_alba" class="mw-redirect" title="Tyto alba">Tyto alba</a></i>) occurs in all three of the islands they are not commonplace. The Cayman Islands also possess five endemic subspecies of butterflies.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> These butterfly breeds can be viewed at the <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II_Botanic_Park" title="Queen Elizabeth II Botanic Park">Queen Elizabeth II Botanic Park</a> on the Grand Cayman.
</p><p>Among other notable fauna at the Queen Elizabeth II Botanic Park is the critically threatened <a href="/wiki/Blue_iguana" title="Blue iguana">blue iguana</a> which is also known as the Grand Cayman iguana (<i>Cyclura lewisi</i>). The blue iguana is endemic to the Grand Cayman<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> particularly because of rocky, sunlit, open areas near the island's shores that are advantageous for the laying of eggs. Nevertheless, habitat destruction and invasive mammalian predators remain the primary reasons that blue iguana hatchlings do not survive naturally.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cuban_crocodile" title="Cuban crocodile">Cuban crocodile</a> (<i>Crocodylus rhombifer</i>) once inhabited the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The name "Cayman" is derived from a <a href="/wiki/Cariban_languages" title="Cariban languages">Carib</a> word for various crocodilians.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg/220px-Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="177" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg/330px-Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg/440px-Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2503" data-file-height="2014" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hurricane_signs_rum_point.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Signs at Rum Point commemorating landed and near-miss hurricanes</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_the_Cayman_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of the Cayman Islands">Climate of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The Cayman Islands has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_wet_and_dry_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical wet and dry climate">tropical wet and dry climate</a>, with a wet season from May to October, and a dry season that runs from November to April. Seasonally, there is little temperature change.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A major <a href="/wiki/Natural_hazard" title="Natural hazard">natural hazard</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">tropical cyclones</a> that form during the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_hurricane_season" title="Atlantic hurricane season">Atlantic hurricane season</a> from June to November.
</p><p>On 11 and 12 September 2004, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Ivan" title="Hurricane Ivan">Hurricane Ivan</a> struck the Cayman Islands. The storm resulted in two deaths and caused significant damage to the infrastructure on the islands. The total economic impact of the storms was estimated to be $3.4 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Demographics of the Cayman Islands">Demographics of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>According to the Economics and Statistics Office of the Government of the Cayman Islands, the Cayman Islands had a population of 78,554 as of fall 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> It was revealed in the 2021 census that 56% of the workforce is Non-Caymanian; this is the first time in the territory's history that the number of working immigrants has overtaken the number of working Caymanians.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Most Caymanians are of mixed African and European ancestry. Slavery was not common throughout the islands, and once it was abolished, communities seemed to integrate more compliantly than other Caribbean nations and territories, resulting in a more mixed-race population.
</p><p>The country's demographics are changing rapidly. Immigration plays a huge role, however, the changing demographics in age have sounded alarm bells in the most recent census. In comparison to the 2010 census, the 2021 census has shown that 36% of Cayman's population growth has been in seniors over the age of 65, while only 8% growth was recorded in groups under 15 years of age. This is due to extremely low birth rates among Caymanians, which almost forces the government to have to seek out workers from overseas to sustain the country's economic success. This has raised concerns, however, among many young Caymanians, who worry about the workforce becoming increasingly competitive with the influx of workers, as well as rent and property prices going up.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because the population has skyrocketed over the last decade, the Premier of the Cayman Islands, Wayne Panton, has stressed that the islands need more careful and managed growth. Many have worried that the country's infrastructure and services cannot cope with the surging population.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="District_populations">District populations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: District populations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg/220px-Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg/330px-Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg/440px-Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2337" data-file-height="1559" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Traditional_caymanian_home_east_end.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Traditional Caymanian home at East End, Grand Cayman</div></div></div>
<p>According to a 2022 population report by the Cayman Islands Government, the estimated population is 78,554,<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> however, the permanent resident population is 71,105,<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> broken down as follows:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/George_Town,_Cayman_Islands" title="George Town, Cayman Islands">George Town</a>: 34,921</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/West_Bay,_Cayman_Islands" title="West Bay, Cayman Islands">West Bay</a>: 15,335</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bodden_Town_(village)" title="Bodden Town (village)">Bodden Town</a>: 14,845</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/North_Side,_Cayman_Islands" title="North Side, Cayman Islands">North Side</a>: 1,902</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/East_End,_Cayman_Islands" title="East End, Cayman Islands">East End</a>: 1,846</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Cayman_Brac" title="Cayman Brac">Cayman Brac</a> and <a href="/wiki/Little_Cayman" title="Little Cayman">Little Cayman</a> (Sister Islands): 2,257<sup id="cite_ref-auto_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span id="Demographics_.26_immigration"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics_&amp;_immigration">Demographics &amp; immigration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Demographics &amp; immigration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>According to the Cayman Islands Spring 2022 Labour Force report, 48.9% of the country is Caymanian. This includes generational Caymanians and those who have obtained Caymanian status (term used for citizenship).<sup id="cite_ref-demographic_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-demographic-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The remaining 51.1% are immigrants on work permits, or those with permanent residency, which is a pathway to gaining status. The Cayman Islands has long been a popular destination for expatriates, dating back to the late 1970s when the country's financial sector started to take off.
</p>
