<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The predominant religion on the Cayman Islands is <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a><sup id="cite_ref-:0_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> (66.9%, down from over 80% in 2010).<sup id="cite_ref-eso-ky-2021_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eso-ky-2021-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Religions practised include United Church, Church of God, Anglican Church, Baptist Church, Catholic Church, Seventh-day Adventist Church, and Pentecostal Church. Roman Catholic churches are St. Ignatius Church, George Town, Christ the Redeemer Church, West Bay and Stella Maris Church, Cayman Brac. Many citizens are deeply religious, regularly going to church, however, atheism has been on the rise throughout the islands since 2000, with 16.7% now identifying with no religion, according to the 2021 census. Ports are closed on Sundays and Christian holidays. There is also an active synagogue and Jewish community<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> on the island as well as places of worship in George Town for <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> and followers of the Bahá'í faith.
</p><p>In 2020, there are an estimated 121 <a href="/wiki/Muslims" title="Muslims">muslims</a> in Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Elmslie_United_Church_(5193988328).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg/220px-Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg/330px-Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg/440px-Elmslie_United_Church_%285193988328%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Elmslie_United_Church_(5193988328).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Elmslie United Church, George Town</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The official language of the Cayman Islands is English (90%).<sup id="cite_ref-:0_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Islanders' accents retain elements passed down from English, Scottish, and Welsh settlers (among others) in a language variety known as Cayman Creole. Caymanians of Jamaican origin speak in their own vernacular (see <a href="/wiki/Jamaican_Patois" title="Jamaican Patois">Jamaican Creole</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jamaican_English" title="Jamaican English">Jamaican English</a>). It is also quite commonplace to hear some residents converse in Spanish<sup id="cite_ref-:0_56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> as many citizens have relocated from Latin America to work and live on Grand Cayman. The Latin American nations with the greatest representation are Honduras, Cuba, Colombia, Nicaragua, and the Dominican Republic. Spanish speakers comprise approximately between 10 and 12% of the population and are predominantly of the Caribbean dialect. <a href="/wiki/Tagalog_language" title="Tagalog language">Tagalog</a> is spoken by about 5% of inhabitants most of whom are Filipino residents on work permits.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_56-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Economy of the Cayman Islands">Economy of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cayman_Islands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Cayman Islands exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>The economy of the Cayman Islands is dominated by financial services and tourism, together accounting for 50-60% of <a href="/wiki/Gross_Domestic_Product" class="mw-redirect" title="Gross Domestic Product">Gross Domestic Product</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The nation's low tax rates have led to it being used as a <a href="/wiki/Tax_haven" title="Tax haven">tax haven</a> for corporations; there are 100,000 companies registered in the Cayman Islands, more than the population itself. The Cayman Islands have come under criticism for allegations of <a href="/wiki/Money_laundering" title="Money laundering">money laundering</a> and other financial crimes, including a 2016 statement by former US president Barack Obama that described a particular building which was the registered address of over 12,000 corporations as a "tax scam".<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Cayman Islands holds a relatively low unemployment rate of about 4.24% as of 2015,<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> lower than the value of 4.7% that was recorded in 2014.
</p><p>With an average income of US$71,549, Caymanians have the highest standard of living in the Caribbean. According to the <a href="/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook" class="mw-redirect" title="CIA World Factbook">CIA World Factbook</a>, the Cayman Islands' real GDP per capita is the tenth highest in the world, but the CIA's data for Cayman dates to 2018 and is likely to be lower than present-day values.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The territory prints its own currency, the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_dollar" title="Cayman Islands dollar">Cayman Islands dollar</a> (KYD), which is pegged to the <a href="/wiki/US_dollar" class="mw-redirect" title="US dollar">US dollar</a> US$1.227 to 1 KYD. However, in many retail stores throughout the islands, the KYD is typically traded at US$1.25.<sup id="cite_ref-Cayman_Currency_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cayman_Currency-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cayman Islands have a high cost of living, even when compared to UK and US.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> For example, a loaf of multigrain bread is $5.49 (KYD), while a similar loaf sells for $2.47 (KYD) in the US and $1.36 (KYD) in the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The minimum wage (as of February 2021) is $6 KYD for standard positions, and $4.50 for workers in the service industry, where tips supplement income.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> This contributes to wealth disparity.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> A small segment of the population lives in condemned properties lacking power and running water.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government has established a <a href="/wiki/Needs_Assessment_Unit" title="Needs Assessment Unit">Needs Assessment Unit</a> to relieve poverty in the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Local charities, including <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.arkcayman.org/">Cayman's Acts of Random Kindness (ARK)</a> also provide assistance.
</p><p>The government's primary source of income is <a href="/wiki/Indirect_tax" title="Indirect tax">indirect taxation</a>: there is no <a href="/wiki/Income_tax" title="Income tax">income tax</a>, <a href="/wiki/Capital_gains_tax" title="Capital gains tax">capital gains tax</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Corporation_tax" class="mw-redirect" title="Corporation tax">corporation tax</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-biswas_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-biswas-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/Import_duty" class="mw-redirect" title="Import duty">import duty</a> of 5% to 22% (automobiles 29.5% to 100%) is levied against goods imported into the islands. Few goods are exempt; notable exemptions include books, cameras, gold, and perfume.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Scuba diving in the Cayman Islands">Scuba diving in the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tnone" style="margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto;overflow:hidden;width:auto;max-width:1408px"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="noresize" style="overflow:auto"><a href="/wiki/File:Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg" class="image" title="Panorama of Seven Mile Beach on Grand Cayman"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg/1400px-Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg" decoding="async" width="1400" height="115" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg/2100px-Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg/2800px-Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg 2x" data-file-width="11744" data-file-height="968" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Seven_Mile_Beach_2006.jpg" title="File:Seven Mile Beach 2006.jpg"> </a></div><a href="/wiki/Panorama" title="Panorama">Panorama</a> of <a href="/wiki/Seven_Mile_Beach,_Grand_Cayman" title="Seven Mile Beach, Grand Cayman">Seven Mile Beach</a> on <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman" title="Grand Cayman">Grand Cayman</a></div></div></div><p>
One of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Cayman" title="Grand Cayman">Grand Cayman</a>'s main attractions is <a href="/wiki/Seven_Mile_Beach,_Grand_Cayman" title="Seven Mile Beach, Grand Cayman">Seven Mile Beach</a>, site of a number of the island's hotels and resorts. Named one of the Ultimate Beaches by Caribbean Travel and Life, Seven Mile Beach (due to erosion over the years, the number has decreased to 5.5 miles) is a public beach on the western shore of Grand Cayman Island.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Historical sites in Grand Cayman, such as <a href="/wiki/Pedro_St._James_Castle" title="Pedro St. James Castle">Pedro St. James Castle</a> in Savannah, also attract visitors.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> </p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:304px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg/302px-Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="302" height="103" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg/453px-Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg/604px-Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="9078" data-file-height="3086" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Looking_Towards_Seven_Mile_Beach_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Observation Tower at Camana Bay, north George Town</div></div></div><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg/220px-Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg/330px-Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg/440px-Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2907" data-file-height="2180" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stingray_passing_-_Grand_Cayman.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Stingray passing through <a href="/wiki/Stingray_City,_Grand_Cayman" title="Stingray City, Grand Cayman">Stingray City, Grand Cayman</a></div></div></div>
<p>All three islands offer <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">scuba diving</a>, and the Cayman Islands are home to several snorkelling locations where tourists can swim with <a href="/wiki/Stingray" title="Stingray">stingrays</a>. The most popular area to do this is <a href="/wiki/Stingray_City,_Grand_Cayman" title="Stingray City, Grand Cayman">Stingray City, Grand Cayman</a>. Stingray City is a top attraction in Grand Cayman and originally started in the 1980s when divers started feeding squid to stingrays. The stingrays started to associate the sound of the boat motors with food, and thus visit this area year-round.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are two <a href="/wiki/Shipwreck" title="Shipwreck">shipwrecks</a> off the shores of Cayman Brac, including the <a href="/wiki/Sinking_ships_for_wreck_diving_sites#List_of_ships_sunk_for_wreck_diving" title="Sinking ships for wreck diving sites">MV Captain Keith Tibbetts</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Grand Cayman also has several shipwrecks off its shores, including one deliberate one. On 30 September 1994, the <a href="/wiki/USS_Kittiwake_(ASR-13)" title="USS Kittiwake (ASR-13)">USS&#160;<i>Kittiwake</i></a> was decommissioned and struck from the Naval Vessel Register. In November 2008 her ownership was transferred for an undisclosed amount to the government of the Cayman Islands, which had decided to sink the <i>Kittiwake</i> in June 2009 to form a new artificial reef off Seven Mile Beach, Grand Cayman. Following several delays, the ship was finally scuttled according to plan on 5 January 2011. The <i>Kittiwake</i> has become a dynamic environment for marine life. While visitors are not allowed to take anything, there are endless sights. Each of the five decks of the ship offers squirrelfish, rare sponges, Goliath groupers, urchins, and more. Experienced and beginner divers are invited to swim around the <i>Kittiwake</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> <span class="anchor" id="Pirates_Week"></span> Pirates Week is an annual 11-day November festival started in 1977 by the then-Minister of Tourism Jim Bodden to boost tourism during the country's tourism slow season.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other Grand Cayman tourist attractions include the ironshore landscape of <a href="/wiki/Hell,_Grand_Cayman" title="Hell, Grand Cayman">Hell</a>; the 23-acre (93,000&#160;m<sup>2</sup>) marine theme park "<a href="/wiki/Cayman_Turtle_Centre" title="Cayman Turtle Centre">Cayman Turtle Centre</a>: Island Wildlife Encounter", previously known as "Boatswain's Beach"; the production of gourmet <a href="/wiki/Sea_salt" title="Sea salt">sea salt</a>; and the <a href="/wiki/Mastic_Reserve" title="Mastic Reserve">Mastic Trail</a>, a hiking trail through the forests in the centre of the island. The National Trust for the Cayman Islands provides guided tours weekly on the Mastic Trail and other locations.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another attraction to visit on Grand Cayman is the Observation Tower, located in Camana Bay. The Observation Tower is 75 feet tall and provides 360-degree views across Seven Mile Beach, George Town, the North Sound, and beyond. It is free to the public and climbing the tower has become a popular thing to do in the Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Points of interest include the <a href="/wiki/East_End_Light" title="East End Light">East End Light</a> (sometimes called Gorling Bluff Light), a lighthouse at the east end of Grand Cayman island. The lighthouse is the centrepiece of East End Lighthouse Park, managed by the <a href="/wiki/National_Trust_for_the_Cayman_Islands" title="National Trust for the Cayman Islands">National Trust for the Cayman Islands</a>; the first navigational aid on the site was the first lighthouse in the Cayman Islands.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Shipping">Shipping</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Shipping">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As of 31&#160;December&#160;2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 360 commercial vessels and 1,674 pleasure craft were registered in the Cayman Islands totalling 4.3 million <a href="/wiki/Gross_tonnage" title="Gross tonnage">GT</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Labour">Labour</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Labour">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Cayman Islands has a population of 69,656 (as of 2021<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>) and therefore a limited workforce.<sup id="cite_ref-eso_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eso-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Work_permit" title="Work permit">Work permits</a> may, therefore, be granted to foreigners. On average, there have been more than 24,000+ foreigners holding valid work permits.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Work_permits_for_non-citizens">Work permits for non-citizens</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Work permits for non-citizens">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>To work in the Cayman Islands as a <a href="/wiki/Alien_(law)" title="Alien (law)">non-citizen</a>, a work permit is required. This involves passing a police background check and a health check. A prospective immigrant worker will not be granted a permit unless certain medical conditions are met, including testing negative for <a href="/wiki/Syphilis" title="Syphilis">syphilis</a> and <a href="/wiki/HIV" title="HIV">HIV</a>. A permit may be granted to individuals on special work.
</p><p>A foreigner must first have a job to move to the Cayman Islands. The employer applies and pays for the work permit.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Work permits are not granted to foreigners who are in the Cayman Islands (unless it is a renewal). The Cayman Islands Immigration Department requires foreigners to remain out of the country until their work permit has been approved.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Cayman Islands presently imposes a controversial "rollover" in relation to <a href="/wiki/Expatriate" title="Expatriate">expatriate</a> workers who require a work permit. Non-Caymanians are only permitted to reside and work within the territory for a maximum of nine years unless they satisfy the criteria of key employees. Non-Caymanians who are "rolled over" may return to work for additional nine-year periods, subject to a one-year gap between their periods of work. The policy has been the subject of some controversy within the press. Law firms have been particularly upset by the recruitment difficulties that it has caused.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Other less well-remunerated employment sectors have been affected as well. Concerns about safety have been expressed by diving instructors, and realtors have also expressed concerns. Others support the rollover as necessary to protect Caymanian identity in the face of immigration of large numbers of expatriate workers.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Concerns have been expressed that in the long term, the policy may damage the preeminence of the Cayman Islands as an <a href="/wiki/Offshore_financial_centre" title="Offshore financial centre">offshore financial centre</a> by making it difficult to recruit and retain experienced staff from onshore financial centres. Government employees are no longer exempt from this "rollover" policy, according to this report in a local newspaper.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> The governor has used his constitutional powers, which give him absolute control over the disposition of <a href="/wiki/Civil_service" title="Civil service">civil service</a> employees, to determine which expatriate civil servants are dismissed after seven years service and which are not.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>This policy is incorporated in the Immigration Law (2003 revision), written by the United Democratic Party government, and subsequently enforced by the People's Progressive Movement Party government. Both governments agree to the term limits on foreign workers, and the majority of Caymanians also agree it is necessary to protect local culture and heritage from being eroded by a large number of foreigners gaining residency and citizenship.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="CARICOM_Single_Market_Economy">CARICOM Single Market Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: CARICOM Single Market Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In recognition of the CARICOM (Free Movement) Skilled Persons Act which came into effect in July 1997 in some of the <a href="/wiki/CARICOM" class="mw-redirect" title="CARICOM">CARICOM</a> countries such as Jamaica and which has been adopted in other CARICOM countries, such as Trinidad and Tobago<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> it is possible that CARICOM nationals who hold the "A Certificate of Recognition of Caribbean Community Skilled Person" will be allowed to work in the Cayman Islands<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> under normal working conditions.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Politics of the Cayman Islands">Politics of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The Cayman Islands are a <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">British overseas territory</a>, listed by the UN Special Committee of 24 as one of the 16 <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">non-self-governing territories</a>. The current Constitution, incorporating a Bill of Rights, was ordained by a <a href="/wiki/Statutory_Instrument_(UK)" class="mw-redirect" title="Statutory Instrument (UK)">statutory instrument</a> of the United Kingdom in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> A 19-seat (not including two non-voting members appointed by the Governor which brings the total to 21 members) <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Parliament of the Cayman Islands">Parliament</a> is elected by the people every four years to handle domestic affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Of the elected Members of the Parliament (MPs), seven are chosen to serve as government Ministers in a Cabinet headed by the Governor. The <a href="/wiki/Premier" title="Premier">Premier</a> is appointed by the Governor.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Governor of the Cayman Islands">Governor</a> is appointed by the <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Monarchy of the United Kingdom">King</a> of the United Kingdom on the advice of the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Government of the United Kingdom">British Government</a> to represent the monarch.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Governors can exercise complete legislative and executive authority if they wish through blanket powers reserved to them in the constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Bills which have passed the Parliament require <a href="/wiki/Royal_assent" title="Royal assent">royal assent</a> before becoming effective. The Constitution empowers the Governor to withhold <a href="/wiki/Royal_assent" title="Royal assent">royal assent</a> in cases where the legislation appears to him or her to be repugnant to or inconsistent with the Constitution or affects the rights and privileges of the Parliament or the Royal Prerogative, or matters reserved to the Governor by article 55.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The executive authority of the Cayman Islands is vested in <a href="/wiki/Charles_III" title="Charles III">the King</a> and is exercised by the Government, consisting of the Governor and the Cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> There is an office of the Deputy Governor, who must be a Caymanian and have served in a senior public office. The Deputy Governor is the acting Governor when the office of Governor is vacant, or the Governor is not able to discharge his or her duties or is absent from the Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The current Governor of the Cayman Islands is <a href="/wiki/Martyn_Roper" title="Martyn Roper">Martyn Roper</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Cabinet is composed of two official members and seven elected members, called Ministers; one of whom is designated <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Premier of the Cayman Islands">Premier</a>. The Premier can serve for two consecutive terms after which he or she is barred from attaining the office again. Although an MP can only be Premier twice any person who meets the qualifications and requirements for a seat in the Parliament can be elected to the Parliament indefinitely.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are two official members of the Parliament, the <a href="/wiki/Deputy_governor" title="Deputy governor">Deputy Governor</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Attorney_general" title="Attorney general">Attorney General</a>. They are appointed by the Governor in accordance with His Majesty's instructions, and although they have seats in the Parliament, under the 2009 Constitution, they do not vote. They serve in a professional and advisory role to the MPs, the Deputy Governor represents the Governor who is a representative of the King and the British Government. While the Attorney General serves to advise on legal matters and has special responsibilities in Parliament, they are generally responsible for changes to the Penal code.
</p><p>The seven Ministers are voted into office by the 19 elected members of the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Parliament of the Cayman Islands">Parliament of the Cayman Islands</a>. One of the Ministers, the leader of the majority political party, is appointed Premier by the Governor.
</p><p>After consulting the Premier, the Governor allocates a portfolio of responsibilities to each Cabinet Minister. Under the principle of collective responsibility, all Ministers are obliged to support in the Parliament any measures approved by Cabinet.
</p><p>Almost 80 departments, sections and units carry out the business of government, joined by a number of statutory boards and authorities set up for specific purposes, such as the Port Authority, the Civil Aviation Authority, the Immigration Board, the Water Authority, the University College Board of Governors, the National Pensions Board and the Health Insurance Commission.
</p><p>Since 2000, there have been two official major political parties: The <a href="/wiki/United_Democratic_Party_(Cayman_Islands)" class="mw-redirect" title="United Democratic Party (Cayman Islands)">Cayman Democratic Party</a> (CDP) and the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Progressive_Movement_(Cayman_Islands)" title="People&#39;s Progressive Movement (Cayman Islands)">People's Progressive Movement</a> (PPM). While there has been a shift to political parties, many contending for office still run as independents. The two parties are notably similar, though they consider each other rivals in most cases, their differences are generally in personality and implementation rather than actual policy. The Cayman Islands generally lacks any form of organised political parties.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> As of the May 2017 General Election, members of the PPM and CDP have joined with three independent members to form a government coalition despite many years of enmity.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Police">Police</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Police">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Royal_Cayman_Islands_Police_Service" title="Royal Cayman Islands Police Service">Royal Cayman Islands Police Service</a></div>
<p>Policing in the country is provided chiefly by the RCIPS or <a href="/wiki/Royal_Cayman_Islands_Police_Service" title="Royal Cayman Islands Police Service">Royal Cayman Islands Police Service</a> and the CICBC or Cayman Islands Customs &amp; Border Control. These two agencies co-operate in aspects of law enforcement, including their <a href="/wiki/Royal_Cayman_Islands_Police_Service#Joint_Marine_Unit" title="Royal Cayman Islands Police Service">joint marine unit</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_defence">Military and defence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Military and defence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The defence of the Cayman Islands is the responsibility of the United Kingdom. The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> maintains a ship on permanent station in the Caribbean (<a href="/wiki/HMS_Medway_(P223)" title="HMS Medway (P223)">HMS Medway (P223)</a>) and additionally sends another <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> or <a href="/wiki/Royal_Fleet_Auxiliary" title="Royal Fleet Auxiliary">Royal Fleet Auxiliary</a> ship as a part of Atlantic Patrol (NORTH) tasking. These ships' main mission in the region is to maintain British sovereignty for the overseas territories, provide humanitarian aid and disaster relief during disasters such as hurricanes, which are common in the area, and to conduct counter-narcotic operations.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Cayman_Islands_Regiment">Cayman Islands Regiment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Cayman Islands Regiment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Regiment" title="Cayman Islands Regiment">Cayman Islands Regiment</a></div>
<p>On 12 October 2019, the government announced the formation of the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Regiment" title="Cayman Islands Regiment">Cayman Islands Regiment</a>, a new <a href="/wiki/British_Armed_Forces" title="British Armed Forces">British Armed Forces</a> unit. The Cayman Islands Regiment which became fully operational in 2020, with an initial 35–50 personnel of mostly reservists. Between 2020 through 2021 the Regiment grew to over a hundred personnel and over the next several years expected to grow to over a several hundred personnel.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-December 2019, recruitment for commanding officers and junior officers began, with the commanding officers expected to begin work in January 2020 and the junior officers expected to begin in February 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 2020, the first officers were chosen for the Cayman Islands Regiment.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the formation of the Regiment, it has been deployed on a few operational tours providing HADR or Humanitarian Aid and Disaster Relief as well as assisting with the COVid19 Pandemic.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Cadet_Corps">Cadet Corps</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Cadet Corps">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Cadet_Corps" title="Cayman Islands Cadet Corps">Cayman Islands Cadet Corps</a> was formed in March 2001 and carries out military-type training with teenage citizens of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Coast_Guard">Coast Guard</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Coast Guard">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Coast_Guard" title="Cayman Islands Coast Guard">Cayman Islands Coast Guard</a></div>
<p>In 2018, the PPM-led Coalition government pledged to form a <a href="/wiki/Coast_guard" title="Coast guard">coast guard</a> to protect the interests of the Cayman Islands, especially in terms of illegal immigration and illegal drug importation as well as <a href="/wiki/Search_and_rescue" title="Search and rescue">search and rescue</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> In mid-2018, the Commander and second-in-Command of the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Coast_Guard" title="Cayman Islands Coast Guard">Cayman Islands Coast Guard</a> were appointed. Commander Robert Scotland was appointed as the first commanding officer and Lieutenant Commander Leo Anglin was appointed as Second-in-Command.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-2019, the commander and second-in-command took part in international joint operations with the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Coast_Guard" title="United States Coast Guard">United States Coast Guard</a> and the <a href="/wiki/JDF_Coast_Guard" class="mw-redirect" title="JDF Coast Guard">Jamaica Defense Force Coast Guard</a> called Operation Riptide. This makes it the first deployment for the Cayman Islands Coast Guard and the first in ten years any Cayman Representative has been on a foreign military ship for a counternarcotic operation.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In late November 2019, it was announced that the Cayman Islands Coast Guard would become operational in January 2020, with initial total of 21 Coast Guardsmen half of which would come from the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Cayman_Islands_Police_Service#Joint_Marine_Unit" title="Royal Cayman Islands Police Service">joint marine unit</a>, with further recruitment in the new year. One of the many taskings of the Coast Guard will be to push enforcement of all laws that apply to the designated Wildlife Interaction Zone.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 5 October 2021, the Cayman Islands Parliament passed the Cayman Islands Coast Guard Act thus establishing the Cayman Islands Coast Guard as a uniformed and disciplined department of Government.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Taxation">Taxation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Taxation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>No direct taxation is imposed on residents and Cayman Islands companies. The government receives the majority of its income from indirect taxation. Duty is levied against most imported goods, which is typically in the range of 22% to 25%. Some items are exempted, such as baby formula, books, cameras, <a href="/wiki/Electric_vehicles" class="mw-redirect" title="Electric vehicles">electric vehicles</a> and certain items are taxed at 5%. Duty on automobiles depends on their value. The duty can amount to 29.5% up to $20,000.00 KYD CIF (cost, insurance and freight) and up to 42% over $30,000.00 KYD CIF for expensive models. The government charges flat licensing fees on financial institutions that operate in the islands and there are work permit fees on foreign labour. A 13% government tax is placed on all tourist accommodations in addition to a US$37.50 airport departure tax which is built into the cost of an airline ticket. There is a 7.5% sales tax on the proceeds of the sale of the property, payable by the purchaser. There are no taxes on corporate profits, capital gains, or personal income. There are no estate or death inheritance taxes payable on Cayman Islands real estate or other assets held in the Cayman Islands.
</p><p>The legend behind the lack of taxation comes from the <a href="/wiki/Wreck_of_the_Ten_Sail" title="Wreck of the Ten Sail">Wreck of the Ten Sail</a>, when multiple ships ran aground on the reef off the north coast of Grand Cayman. Local fishermen are said to have then sailed out to rescue the crew and salvage goods from the wrecks. It is said that out of gratitude, and due to their small size, King George III then issued the edict that the citizens of the country of the Cayman Islands would never pay tax.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> There is, however, no documented evidence for this story besides oral tradition.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:142px;"><a href="/wiki/File:1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg/140px-1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg" decoding="async" width="140" height="206" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg/210px-1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg/280px-1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg 2x" data-file-width="318" data-file-height="467" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:1953caymanislands1poundelizabethIIblue.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Postage stamp with a portrait of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Queen Elizabeth II">Queen Elizabeth II</a>, 1953</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Foreign relations of the Cayman Islands">Foreign relations of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>Foreign policy is controlled by the United Kingdom, as the islands remain an overseas territory of the United Kingdom. Although in its early days, the Cayman Islands' most important relationships were with Britain and <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a>, in recent years, as a result of economic dependence, a relationship with the United States has developed.
</p><p>Though the Cayman Islands is involved in no major international disputes, they have come under some criticism due to the use of their territory for narcotics trafficking and <a href="/wiki/Money_laundering" title="Money laundering">money laundering</a>. In an attempt to address this, the government entered into the Narcotics Agreement of 1984 and the <a href="/wiki/Mutual_Legal_Assistance_Treaty" class="mw-redirect" title="Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty">Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty</a> of 1986 with the United States, to reduce the use of their facilities associated with these activities. In more recent years, they have stepped up the fight against money laundering, by limiting banking secrecy, introducing requirements for customer identification and record keeping, and requiring banks to co-operate with foreign investigators.
</p><p>Due to their status as an overseas territory of the UK, the Cayman Islands has no separate representation either in the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> or in most other international organisations. However, the Cayman Islands still participates in some international organisations, being an associate member of <a href="/wiki/Caricom" class="mw-redirect" title="Caricom">Caricom</a> and <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>, and a member of a sub-bureau of <a href="/wiki/Interpol" title="Interpol">Interpol</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Emergency_Services">Emergency Services</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Emergency Services">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Access to Emergency Services is available using 9-1-1, the <a href="/wiki/Emergency_telephone_number" title="Emergency telephone number">Emergency telephone number</a>, the same number as is used in Canada and the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> The Cayman Islands Department of Public Safety's Communications Centre processes 9-1-1 and non-emergency Police assistance, Ambulance Service, Fire Service and <a href="/wiki/Search_and_rescue" title="Search and rescue">search and rescue</a> calls for all three islands. The Communications Centre dispatches RCIP and EMS units directly; the Cayman Islands Fire Service maintains their own dispatch room at the airport fire station.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Police Services are handled by the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Cayman_Islands_Police_Service" title="Royal Cayman Islands Police Service">Royal Cayman Islands Police Service</a>. The Fire Services are handled by the Cayman Islands Fire Service. There are 4 main hospitals in the Cayman Islands, Private and Public <a href="/wiki/Health_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Health in the Cayman Islands">Health in the Cayman Islands</a> with various localised health clinics around the islands.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Transport in the Cayman Islands">Transport in the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ports">Ports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Ports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>George Town is the port capital of Grand Cayman. There are no berthing facilities for cruise ships, but up to four cruise ships can anchor in designated anchorages. There are three cruise terminals in George Town, the North, South, and Royal Watler Terminals. The ride from the ship to the terminal is about 5 minutes.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Airports_and_airlines">Airports and airlines</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Airports and airlines">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There are three airports which serve the Cayman Islands. The islands' national flag carrier is <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Airways" title="Cayman Airways">Cayman Airways</a>, with Owen Roberts International Airport hosting the airline as its hub.
</p><p>• <a href="/wiki/Owen_Roberts_International_Airport" title="Owen Roberts International Airport">Owen Roberts International Airport</a>
• <a href="/wiki/Charles_Kirkconnell_International_Airport" title="Charles Kirkconnell International Airport">Charles Kirkconnell International Airport</a>
• <a href="/wiki/Edward_Bodden_Airfield" title="Edward Bodden Airfield">Edward Bodden Airfield</a>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Main_Highways">Main Highways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Main Highways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There are three highways, as well as crucial feeder roads that serve the Cayman Islands capital city, George Town. Residents in the east of the city will rely on the East-West Arterial Bypass to go into George Town; as well as Shamrock Road coming from Bodden Town and the eastern districts.
Other main highways and carriageways include:
</p><p>• Linford Pierson Highway (most popular roadway into George Town from the east)
• Esterly Tibbetts Highway (serves commuters to the north of the city and West Bay)
• North Sound Road (main road for Central George Town)
• South Sound Road (used by commuters to the south of the city)
• Crewe Road (alternative to taking Linford Pierson Highway)
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Education in the Cayman Islands">Education in the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Primary_and_secondary_schools">Primary and secondary schools</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Primary and secondary schools">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_schools_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="List of schools in the Cayman Islands">List of schools in the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Education_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="Cayman Islands Education Department">Cayman Islands Education Department</a> operates state schools. Caymanian children are entitled to free primary and secondary education. There are two public high schools on Grand Cayman, <a href="/wiki/John_Gray_High_School" title="John Gray High School">John Gray High School</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clifton_Hunter_High_School" title="Clifton Hunter High School">Clifton Hunter High School</a>, and one on Cayman Brac, <a href="/wiki/Layman_E._Scott_High_School" class="mw-redirect" title="Layman E. Scott High School">Layman E. Scott High School</a>. Various churches and private foundations operate several private schools.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Colleges_and_universities">Colleges and universities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Colleges and universities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/University_College_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="University College of the Cayman Islands">University College of the Cayman Islands</a> has campuses on Grand Cayman and Cayman Brac and is the only government-run university on the Cayman Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/International_College_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="International College of the Cayman Islands">International College of the Cayman Islands</a> is a private college in Grand Cayman. The college was established in 1970 and offers associate's, bachelor's and master's degree programmes.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Grand Cayman is also home to <a href="/wiki/St._Matthew%27s_University" title="St. Matthew&#39;s University">St. Matthew's University</a>, which includes a <a href="/wiki/Medical_school" title="Medical school">medical school</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Veterinary_school" class="mw-redirect" title="Veterinary school">school of veterinary medicine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Law_School" class="mw-redirect" title="Cayman Islands Law School">Cayman Islands Law School</a>, a branch of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Liverpool" title="University of Liverpool">University of Liverpool</a>, is based on Grand Cayman.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/The_Cayman_Islands_Civil_Service_College" class="mw-redirect" title="The Cayman Islands Civil Service College">The Cayman Islands Civil Service College</a>, a unit of the Cayman Islands government organised under the Portfolio of the Civil Service, is in Grand Cayman. Co-situated with University College of the Cayman Islands, it offers both degree programs and continuing education units of various sorts. The college opened in 2007 and is also used as a government research centre.
</p><p>There is a <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_West_Indies" title="University of the West Indies">University of the West Indies</a> Open campus in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Football_in_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Football in the Cayman Islands">Football in the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Truman_Bodden_Stadium" class="mw-redirect" title="Truman Bodden Stadium">Truman Bodden Sports Complex</a> is a multi-use complex in George Town. The complex is separated into an outdoor, six-lane 25-metre (82&#160;ft) swimming pool, full purpose track and field and basketball/netball courts. The field surrounded by the track is used for <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a> matches as well as other field sports. The track stadium holds 3,000 people.
</p><p>Association football is the national and most popular sport, with the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_national_football_team" title="Cayman Islands national football team">Cayman Islands national football team</a> representing the Cayman Islands in <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Cayman Islands Basketball Federation joined the international basketball governing body <a href="/wiki/FIBA" title="FIBA">FIBA</a> in 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The country's <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Cayman Islands national basketball team">national team</a> attended the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Basketball_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="Caribbean Basketball Championship">Caribbean Basketball Championship</a> for the first time in 2011. Cayman Islands National Male National Team has won back 2 back Gold Medal victories in 2017 and 2019 <a href="/wiki/Natwest_Island_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="Natwest Island Games">Natwest Island Games</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Rugby_Union_in_the_Cayman_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Rugby Union in the Cayman Islands">Rugby union</a> is a developing sport, and has its own <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_national_rugby_union_team" title="Cayman Islands national rugby union team">national men's team</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_women%27s_national_rugby_union_team" title="Cayman Islands women&#39;s national rugby union team">women's team</a>, and Sevens team. The Cayman Men's Rugby 7s team is second in the region after the 2011 NACRA 7s Championship.
</p><p>The Cayman Islands are a member of <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a>, the <a href="/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee" title="International Olympic Committee">International Olympic Committee</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Pan_American_Sports_Organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Pan American Sports Organisation">Pan American Sports Organisation</a>, and also competes in the biennial <a href="/wiki/Island_Games" title="Island Games">Island Games</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Cayman Islands are a member of the <a href="/wiki/International_Cricket_Council" title="International Cricket Council">International Cricket Council</a> which they joined in 1997 as an Affiliate, before becoming an Associate member in 2002. The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_national_cricket_team" title="Cayman Islands national cricket team">Cayman Islands national cricket team</a> represents the islands in <a href="/wiki/International_cricket" title="International cricket">international cricket</a>. The team has previously played the sport at <a href="/wiki/First-class_cricket" title="First-class cricket">first-class</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_A_cricket" title="List A cricket">List A</a> and <a href="/wiki/Twenty20_cricket" class="mw-redirect" title="Twenty20 cricket">Twenty20</a> level. It competes in <a href="/wiki/2012_ICC_World_Cricket_League_Division_Five" title="2012 ICC World Cricket League Division Five">Division Five</a> of the <a href="/wiki/World_Cricket_League" title="World Cricket League">World Cricket League</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ICCP_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ICCP-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Squash is popular in the Cayman Islands with a vibrant community of mostly ex-pats playing out of the 7-court South Sound Squash Club. In addition, the women's professional squash association hosts one of their major events each year in an all-glass court being set up in Camana Bay. In December 2012, the former Cayman Open will be replaced by the Women's World Championships, the largest tournament in the world. The top Cayman men's player, Cameron Stafford is No. 2 in the Caribbean and ranked top 200 on the men's professional circuit.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Flag_football" title="Flag football">Flag football</a> (CIFFA) has men's, women's, and mixed-gender leagues.
</p><p>Other organised sports leagues include softball, beach volleyball, <a href="/wiki/Gaelic_football" title="Gaelic football">Gaelic football</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ultimate_frisbee" class="mw-redirect" title="Ultimate frisbee">ultimate frisbee</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands_Olympic_Committee" title="Cayman Islands Olympic Committee">Cayman Islands Olympic Committee</a> was founded in 1973 and was recognised by the IOC (<a href="/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee" title="International Olympic Committee">International Olympic Committee</a>) in 1976.
</p><p>In April 2005 Black Pearl Skate Park was opened in Grand Cayman by <a href="/wiki/Tony_Hawk" title="Tony Hawk">Tony Hawk</a>. At the time the 52,000 square feet (4,800&#160;m<sup>2</sup>) park was the largest in the Western Hemisphere.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2010, the first purpose-built track for <a href="/wiki/Kart_racing" title="Kart racing">kart racing</a> in the Cayman Islands was opened.<sup id="cite_ref-Compass_Track_Opening_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Compass_Track_Opening-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Corporate karting Leagues at the track have involved widespread participation with 20 local companies and 227 drivers taking part in the 2010 Summer Corporate Karting League.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Cydonie_Mothersille" title="Cydonie Mothersille">Cydonie Mothersille</a> was the first track and field athlete from the country to make an Olympic final at the <a href="/wiki/2008_Olympic_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 Olympic Games">2008 Olympic Games</a>. She also won a bronze medal in the <a href="/wiki/200m" class="mw-redirect" title="200m">200m</a> at the <a href="/wiki/2001_World_Championships_in_Athletics" title="2001 World Championships in Athletics">2001 World Championships in Athletics</a> and gold in the same event at the <a href="/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games" title="2010 Commonwealth Games">2010 Commonwealth Games</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Arts_and_culture">Arts and culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Arts and culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="Music of the Cayman Islands">Music of the Cayman Islands</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Cayman_National_Cultural_Foundation" title="Cayman National Cultural Foundation">Cayman National Cultural Foundation</a> manages the F.J. Harquail Cultural Centre and the US$4&#160;million Harquail Theatre. The Cayman National Cultural Foundation, established in 1984, helps to preserve and promote Cayman folk music, including the organisation of festivals such as the Cayman Islands International Storytelling Festival, the Cayman JazzFest, Seafarers Festival and Cayfest.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Jazz" title="Jazz">jazz</a>, <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reggae_music" class="mw-redirect" title="Reggae music">reggae</a> genres of music styles feature prominently in Cayman music as celebrated cultural influences.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Art">Art</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Art">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_of_the_Cayman_Islands" title="National Gallery of the Cayman Islands">National Gallery of the Cayman Islands</a> is an <a href="/wiki/Art_museum" title="Art museum">art museum</a> in <a href="/wiki/George_Town,_Cayman_Islands" title="George Town, Cayman Islands">George Town</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Founded in 1996, NGCI is an arts organisation that seeks to fulfil its mission through exhibitions, artist residencies, education/outreach programmes and research projects in the Cayman Islands. The NGCI is a non-profit institution, part of the Ministry of Health and Culture.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>There are two print newspapers currently in circulation throughout the islands: the <i>Cayman Compass</i> and <i>The Caymanian Times</i>. Online news services include Cayman Compass, Cayman News Service, Cayman Marl Road, The Caymanian Times and Real Cayman News.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Olive_Hilda_Miller" title="Olive Hilda Miller">Olive Hilda Miller</a> was the first paid reporter to work for a Cayman Islands newspaper, beginning her career on the <i>Tradewinds</i> newspaper, which her work helped to establish.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:2_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_Cayman_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="List of radio stations in Cayman Islands">Local radio stations</a> are broadcast throughout the islands.
</p><p>Feature films that have been filmed in the Cayman Islands include: <i><a href="/wiki/The_Firm_(1993_film)" title="The Firm (1993 film)">The Firm</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Haven_(film)" title="Haven (film)">Haven</a></i>, <i>Cayman Went</i><sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> and <i>Zombie Driftwood</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Television in the Cayman Islands consist of three over-the-air broadcast stations, Trinity Broadcasting Network - CIGTV (the government-owned channel) - Seventh Day Adventist Network. Cable television is available in the Cayman Islands through three providers, C3 Pure Fibre - FLOW TV - Logic TV. Satellite television is provided by Dish Direct TV.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Broadband is widely available on the Cayman Islands, with Digicel, C3 Pure Fibre, FLOW and Logic all providing super fast fibre broadband to the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_Caymanians">Notable Caymanians</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cayman_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Notable Caymanians">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li><a href="/wiki/Gladwyn_Bush" title="Gladwyn Bush">Gladwyn K. Bush</a>, folk artist</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/McKeeva_Bush" title="McKeeva Bush">McKeeva Bush</a>, politician</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marc_Chin" title="Marc Chin">Marc Chin</a>, cricketer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_Warren_Conolly" title="William Warren Conolly">William Warren Conolly</a>, politician and national hero</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kenneth_Dart" title="Kenneth Dart">Kenneth Dart</a>, businessman</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Selita_Ebanks" title="Selita Ebanks">Selita Ebanks</a>, fashion model</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Frank_E._Flowers" title="Frank E. Flowers">Frank E. Flowers</a>, filmmaker, director and screenwriter</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marshall_Forbes" title="Marshall Forbes">Marshall Forbes</a>, footballer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ronald_Forbes" title="Ronald Forbes">Ronald Forbes</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brett_Fraser" title="Brett Fraser">Brett Fraser</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shaune_Fraser" title="Shaune Fraser">Shaune Fraser</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jason_Gilbert" title="Jason Gilbert">Jason Gilbert</a>, record producer and songwriter</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Sybil_Joyce_Hylton" title="Sybil Joyce Hylton">Sybil Joyce Hylton</a>, national hero</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kemar_Hyman" title="Kemar Hyman">Kemar Hyman</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Reno_Jackson" title="John Reno Jackson">John Reno Jackson</a>, multidisciplinary artist</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson_(Caymanian_politician)" title="Thomas Jefferson (Caymanian politician)">Thomas Jefferson</a>, Caymanian politician</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Sybil_Ione_McLaughlin" class="mw-redirect" title="Sybil Ione McLaughlin">Sybil Ione McLaughlin</a>, national hero</li>
<li>Sir <a href="/wiki/Alden_McLaughlin" title="Alden McLaughlin">Alden McLaughlin</a>, politician and second Caymanian to receive a knighthood</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Edison_Mclean" title="Edison Mclean">Edison Mclean</a>, first Caymanian gold medalist in Olympic skeet, Island Games<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Cydonie_Mothersille" title="Cydonie Mothersille">Cydonie Mothersille</a>, track and field athlete and Olympian</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Edna_Moyle" title="Edna Moyle">Edna Moyle</a>, former Speaker of the House</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bernard_K._Passman" title="Bernard K. Passman">Bernard K. Passman</a>, jeweller, founded his business on Grand Cayman in 1975</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lee_Ramoon" title="Lee Ramoon">Lee Ramoon</a>, footballer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/David_Ritch" title="David Ritch">David Ritch</a>, attorney and Chairman of the Board of Directors of <a href="/wiki/CIBC_FirstCaribbean_International_Bank" title="CIBC FirstCaribbean International Bank">CIBC FirstCaribbean International Bank</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Leila_Ross-Shier" title="Leila Ross-Shier">Leila Ross-Shier</a>, musician, educator and composer of "<a href="/wiki/Beloved_Isle_Cayman" title="Beloved Isle Cayman">Beloved Isle Cayman</a>"</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Raegan_Rutty" title="Raegan Rutty">Raegan Rutty</a>, Olympic gymnast</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Cameron_Stafford" title="Cameron Stafford">Cameron Stafford</a>, 2010 Caribbean Junior Squash Champion</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kareem_Streete-Thompson" title="Kareem Streete-Thompson">Kareem Streete-Thompson</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tanya_Streeter" title="Tanya Streeter">Tanya Streeter</a>, free-diver</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/The_Barefoot_Man" title="The Barefoot Man">The Barefoot Man</a>, folk singer and national icon</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kurt_Tibbetts" title="Kurt Tibbetts">Kurt Tibbetts</a>, politician</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dow_Travers" title="Dow Travers">Dow Travers</a>, Olympic athlete</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jeffrey_Webb_(football_executive)" title="Jeffrey Webb (football executive)">Jeffrey Webb</a>, former <a href="/wiki/CONCACAF" title="CONCACAF">CONCACAF</a> president and <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> vice-president</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Michael_Wight" title="Michael Wight">Michael Wight</a>, cricketer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mary_Evelyn_Wood" title="Mary Evelyn Wood">Mary Evelyn Wood</a>, national hero</li></ul></div>

<li id="cite_note-45"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-45">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.gov.ky/portal/page/portal/cighome/cayman/islands/history">"The Cayman Islands – History"</a>. <i>Gov.ky</i>. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20180909232014/http://www.gov.ky/portal/page/portal/cighome/cayman/islands/history">Archived</a> from the original on 9 September 2018<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">17 August</span> 2018</span>. <q>A 1523 map show[s] all three Islands with the name Lagartos, meaning alligators or large lizards, but by 1530 the name Caymanas was being used. It is derived from the Carib Indian word for the marine crocodile, which is now known to have lived in the Islands.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Gov.ky&amp;rft.atitle=The+Cayman+Islands+%E2%80%93+History&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.gov.ky%2Fportal%2Fpage%2Fportal%2Fcighome%2Fcayman%2Fislands%2Fhistory&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ACayman+Islands" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<p><b><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Lat. and Long.</a> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Cayman_Islands&amp;params=19_20_N_81_24_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">19°20′N</span> <span class="longitude">81°24′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">19.333°N 81.400°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">19.333; -81.400</span></span></span></a></span> (George Town)</b>

<ul><li>1.&#160;<a href="/wiki/Condominium_(international_law)" title="Condominium (international law)">Occupied jointly</a> with the United States.</li>
<li>2.&#160;In 1931, Canada and other British <a href="/wiki/Dominion" title="Dominion">dominions</a> obtained self-government through the <a href="/wiki/Statute_of_Westminster_1931" title="Statute of Westminster 1931">Statute of Westminster</a>. See <a href="/wiki/Name_of_Canada" title="Name of Canada">Name of Canada</a>.</li>
<li>3.&#160;Gave up <a href="/wiki/Responsible_government" title="Responsible government">self-rule</a> in 1934, but remained a <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i> Dominion until it <a href="/wiki/Provinces_and_territories_of_Canada" title="Provinces and territories of Canada">joined Canada</a> in 1949.</li></ul>
